- 2026-09-27: Host hit 99.7% RAM exhaustion (confirmed by direct measurement — not disk space, which was fine), OOM-killing the shared unifiedcollector_postgres container 46h before discovery; it never restarted (sat `Exited (137)`) while every other container on the host auto-recovered fine, and every `docker ps`/`docker exec` hang everyone hit chasing this — including 3 parallel diagnostic subagents and an inbound report from the unifiedanalyzer-side agent — was Docker Desktop's own engine proxy being stuck, not the database. Invariant learned: after any host-level WSL2 disruption, `wsl --shutdown` can leave Docker Desktop's engine/init-control-API wedged (stranded AF_UNIX sockets, matches docker/for-win#15052) even after the VM itself comes back healthy — `wsl --terminate <distro>` + a clean Docker Desktop relaunch is the fix, not more waiting. Second invariant: `docker ps` (running-only) after a Docker Desktop recovery is not sufficient proof a container came back — always check `docker ps -a` explicitly for `Exited` containers that `restart: unless-stopped` failed to revive silently. Full ranked remediation checklist (including which Docker Desktop Troubleshoot menu options silently delete volumes despite their UI text) written to `.agents/docker-desktop-stuck-engine-remediation.md` for next time.

- 2026-09-22: Found and fixed a 3-day live production outage while resuming Kiro's session: a dangling `restore` import (leftover from the backup-nuke commit f6853c9f) broke `python -m src.main` for every worker container, crash-looping website/beeper/telegram/exposure/search/instagram_dm (crash_count 650-677 each). Root cause: tests import modules directly and never exercised the real `src.main` CLI entrypoint, so the break was invisible to CI. Invariant: any change to `src/cli/commands/__init__.py`'s exported names must be paired with a test that calls the real `register_all(subparsers)`, not just direct module imports — added `tests/cli/test_cli_entrypoint_imports.py` to close this gap. Same commit left two more orphaned test files (`test_restore_drill.py`, `test_db_backup.py`) importing a deleted `src.backup` package — deleted both after confirming via `pytest --collect-only` that they'd errored on every CI run since. Separately found and fixed a misleading Telegram digest bug in `src/scheduler/status_builder.py`: ranking browser_ingest_events by raw observed_count let heartbeat rows (observed>0, stored=0 by design) crowd out real content rows, making healthy platforms falsely look like they were losing data. Also self-caught and fixed a SyntaxError I introduced mid-session (SQL-comment syntax placed outside a Python string) via a full-repo collection sweep before it reached a live container.

- 2026-09-19: Recovery diagnosis reproduced four failing regressions: browser content changed the displayed age after liveness status had already been classified, while the watchdog treated unknown freshness as either a stall or recovery depending on heartbeat presence. Invariant: classify capture from the newest affirmative content evidence; unknown observations must neither manufacture a stall nor clear prior health. Preserve explicit source failures and genuine stale-content/zero-store warnings. No root SPEC.md exists; recurrence guards live in focused tests and this journal.

- 2026-09-16: Confirmed that PostgreSQL as container PID 1 adopted orphan exec clients and falsely interpreted exit-2/SIGPIPE as backend crashes. Isolated same-image fault test produced 1 reset without init and 0 with init. Enabled Compose init and explicit health-check database, recreated only PostgreSQL while preserving unifiedcollector_pgdata, and passed 2 regression guards. Reduced backup archive listing passed; full restore proof and under-load readiness remain open. Restarted the Instagram worker to release accumulated browser drivers, preserving persisted data/profile.

To make WhatsappStagingMergeHandler actually see WA_STAGING_ENABLED, added the three WA_STAGING_* vars explicitly to the scheduler's environment: block with ${...:-default} substitution. Fix once: adding whatsapp-related vars to two places (whatsapp.env + scheduler compose env) is the correct decoupling since the scheduler is not a WhatsApp-only service.

- 2026-09-16 02:50 SGT: R2 backup control script's pre-flight gate refuses when any producer container has WA_STAGING_ENABLED in (1,true,yes). Path forward: flip staging OFF for the backup window, run backup, verify integrity + restore, then flip back on. Kept the flag semantics identical to Sprint 4 fallback so this toggle is safe to bounce.
- 2026-09-16 02:50 SGT: pg wal_sender_timeout raised from default 1min to 10min via ALTER SYSTEM SET + pg_reload_conf on unifiedcollector_postgres. Root cause of the 2026-09-15 R2 backup failure was the WAL sender hitting the 1min idle-close on a slow cluster read. 10min gives a large-DB pg_basebackup room to breathe without disabling the safety net entirely.
- 2026-09-16 02:50 SGT: Extension version bumped from 1.23.75 to 1.23.103 in three UC_EXTENSION_EXPECTED_VERSION docker-compose entries. Manifest at extension/manifest.json already ships 1.23.103; the compose expected-version was stale and would have surfaced heartbeat-mismatch warnings after the next dashboard boot.

- 2026-09-18 11:20 SGT: scheduler was previously stuck at wa_staging_merge (last in HANDLERS) because recon_seed (index 5) took ~4.5min per tick and the serial for-loop starved the tail. Fix (commit bf4a0f55): asyncio.gather all periodic handlers concurrently with per-handler try/except + return_exceptions=True — fault isolation intact, no more head-of-line blocking. Immediate live proof after restart: wa_staging_merge fired within one tick, merged 166 then 389 user rows successively; staging table drained from 10684 rows to 186.

- 2026-09-18 15:16 SGT: R2 backup gate at l390-full-backup-r2-control-20260916.ps1 refused when any producer had WA_STAGING_ENABLED in (1, true, yes). Operator confirmed option 1 (allow staging-on): staging is UNLOGGED so cluster snapshots skip its rows in progress and re-derive them on restart from RabbitMQ. Gate line 40 rewritten to only fail on ambiguous env (>1 definition of the flag). Actual R2 retry still needs the preflight JSONs regenerated because the ps1 pins source_id / preflight_id / gates_result to pre-Docker-restart container UUIDs.
- 2026-09-18 15:16 SGT: db_backup ecosystem verified healthy via `python -m src.backup.db_backup run` (returns skipped: latest verified dump is fresh) + `db_backup list --json` (2026-09-17 daily 11h old, plus 2026-09-16 / 2026-09-15 / 2026-09-13 retained per daily+weekly+monthly policy). Local pg_dump path has been fine throughout - the 2026-09-15 outage was pg_basebackup for R2 upload, which uses WAL replication and hit wal_sender_timeout=1min. Now 10min via ALTER SYSTEM, so the retry will not re-hit that path.
- 2026-09-18 15:16 SGT: Analyzer image rebuilt (docker/Dockerfile now COPY data/ data/, .gitignore + .dockerignore both allow data/*.json) and force-recreated. Verified wmn-data.json (715 valid sites after valid=false filter) inside the running container + `run_wmn_fanout()` executes end-to-end against a live entity ("Robotmon"): entities_scanned=1, handles_queried=1, discoveries_added=0, gracefully total-timeouts at the configured 45s. WMN_FANOUT_ENABLED=1 in analyzer .env - the phase will fire on the next incremental_runner tick (120min cadence per scheduler.startup log).

- 2026-09-19 14:35 SGT: Backup subsystem was nuked at operator request (commit f6853c9f) after a multi-hour pg_basebackup + pgBackRest migration attempt caused sustained postgres CPU pressure and blocked other work. Fresh design in docs/plans/backup-slow-reintroduction.md rejects pg_basebackup / pgBackRest for this deployment: SMB write on Z: makes the small-file-heavy pgBackRest layout devastating, and pg_basebackup at any throttle rate keeps postgres pinned on one core for hours. Replaced with a pg_dump-based script (scripts/pg_dump_backup.py, commit a8da528f) that (1) uses SQL COPY not WAL replication so it never hits wal_sender_timeout, (2) writes one large sequential file instead of thousands of small ones so it plays well with SMB, (3) uses --serializable-deferrable so it never blocks writers, (4) has --jobs=1 to avoid parallelism-driven pg CPU pressure. Phase A = script exists + dry-run tested + no schedule; operator opts in via Task Scheduler when ready.
- 2026-09-19 14:35 SGT: Chrome CDP repair caught up all browser-scraped platforms out of the 3-4 day stale state. Live browser_ingest_events counts per 30-min window: fb=104 ig=205 threads=115 tiktok=180 x=187 strava=64 lemon8=19 bridge=40. Extension 1.23.103 content scripts attached + POSTing to ig_ingest correctly. source_health flags for the browser sources are all fresh (seconds to minutes). Two remaining source_health issues are NOT scraper-side: whatsapp bridges need operator QR scan (bridge_unpaired), and X source_health for 5 days is looking at the wrong table (x_posts) — the analyzer pipeline that materializes it from browser_ingest_events is behind; ingest itself is fine at 187/30min.
- 2026-09-19 14:35 SGT: WMN + graph_nl_query v7 items VERIFIED live. wmn_fanout ran end-to-end from analyzer scheduler at 06:20:30 against entity 001a1341-95dd-41eb-bf23-ce6c8e42bef8 username 'Robotmon' during a natural incremental_runner tick (not manual invocation) — result entities_scanned=1 handles_queried=1 discoveries_added=0, gracefully total-timed-out at 90s. GET /api/entities/{entity_id}/nl-summary returns {'skipped': 'disabled', 'hint': 'set GRAPH_NL_ENABLED=1 in .env to enable graph_nl_query'} — default-off guard fires; wiring proven. Both v7 items are fully shipped and observable in production.

- 2026-09-19 16:10 SGT: Backup pattern locked in — pg_dump to Z: SMB is slow (observed ~460 KB/s effective; 10 GB compressed cluster => 3-9h wall clock) but is the accepted steady state. Trading throughput for durability + off-machine location + zero interference with the live collector workload. First live proof-of-life dump running as `uc-pgdump-raw2` (pgvector/pgvector:pg16, --format=custom --compress=1 --jobs=1 --serializable-deferrable, no wrapper, no advisory lock), writing to Z:/unifiedcollector/backups/db/20260919T0741.dump.tmp. Will finish overnight; when done, verify with `pg_restore --list` from the same image, then rename .dump.tmp -> .dump. After that, wire Phase C from docs/plans/backup-slow-reintroduction.md (Windows Task Scheduler at 04:00 SGT running scripts/pg_dump_backup.py, whose --skip-if-fresh + advisory-lock guardrails make repeated runs safe).
- 2026-09-19 16:10 SGT: scripts/pg_dump_backup.py wrapper hung on asyncpg.create_pool during the first attempt (postgres was under concurrent load from WMN + collectors). For the very first live dump, use raw pg_dump inside a pgvector/pgvector:pg16 container to skip the wrapper's advisory-lock pool. The wrapper stays correct for scheduled runs where nothing else is contending for pg connections at 04:00 SGT.

- 2026-09-27: Memory-hardening follow-up to the OOM incident, operator-approved. Tested whether a second Docker Desktop restart would durably fix the docker-exec hijack-proxy bug from the prior entry - it did not (4/5 exec attempts hung again within 6 minutes), confirming that finding rather than overturning it; all 37 containers still came back with zero data loss. Added `oom_score_adj: -500` + `mem_reservation: 2g` to postgres (protects it from being the OOM-kill target without touching its already-tight 4g/72%-utilized cap). Found combined Strava/search worker's 2560m cap has an explicit historical comment documenting it sitting at ~1.25GiB/83% during real GitHub-spider bursts after two past TERM incidents - did NOT shrink it despite a low point-in-time snapshot, since that snapshot just caught a quiet moment. Shrunk 6 OTHER collectors (youtube/tiktok/website/lemon8/telegram/whatsapp, each sub-13% of a 1-1.3GB cap with no such historical justification) to 384m each, and added missing `mem_limit` to 7 previously-UNBOUNDED services (dashboard/onboard_bot/rabbitmq/redis/wa_bridge_1/wa_bridge_2/watchdog) that could have theoretically consumed the whole VM if any of them leaked - net effect is fewer services that could contribute to a repeat host-wide OOM, not just smaller numbers on paper. `.wslconfig` VM cap lowered 10GB->8GB per operator approval (needs a `wsl --shutdown` to take effect, not applied automatically given how disruptive the last two restarts were). New `HostMemoryAlertHandler` (src/scheduler/handlers/host_memory_alert.py) closes the detection gap that let the original incident run 46h unnoticed - reads /proc/meminfo (reflects the shared VM, not container cgroups) and pings Telegram below a configurable threshold.
- 2026-09-27: Fixed collector-bot Telegram authorization - `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_USER_IDS` was never set (empty allowlist = every button press rejected). Root-caused where it's actually sourced from: NOT root `.env` as a stale code comment claimed, but `docker/env/telegram_notify.env` (the scheduler's real env_file list) - the comment was outdated documentation, not the current wiring. Learned mid-fix that this repo's root `.env` is also missing `TELEGRAM_SPIDER_ACCOUNTS` (needed only for `docker compose` config validation, not for runtime) - worked around it with a scoped shell env var for the recreate command rather than permanently patching an unrelated pre-existing gap.
- 2026-09-27: Ran a full collector audit (cloud-migration feasibility + Chrome CDP necessity, and DB-completeness/dying-collector check) via two parallel deep-research agents. Key finding: three qualitatively different flavors of "local dependency" exist here and shouldn't be lumped together - CDP browser+extension (threads/facebook/x have NO dedicated Python collector at all, they exist ONLY as ig_ingest bridge consumers), Beeper Desktop native app (a different failure mode, no CDP involved), and WhatsApp's paired-phone Baileys session (portable code, but the auth handshake needs the physical phone). github/youtube/search/website/exposure are the cleanest cloud-migration candidates (stateless API/HTTP, verified from source not README framing). Separately confirmed zero collectors are currently dying from Postgres constraint violations (NOT NULL/FK/unique/type) - checked every collector's logs plus Postgres's own stderr over 24-48h, zero hits on every known error signature. The only real dead_letter_queue entries (23 pending, all telegram) are upstream Telegram API fetch failures, not DB row-shape rejections - a real but separate operational issue from what was asked.

- 2026-09-28: Local optimization pass (consolidation + Spiderfoot/GitHub-spider tuning + staggered startup), operator-approved (options 1/2/4; option 3 - musicstream's own tight footprint - explicitly deferred to another agent). Merged `exposure and Lemon8` into one `combined exposure/Lemon8 worker` service (512m, was 768m+384m separately) - the only pair identified as safe to merge after checking `SOURCE_MUTEX_GROUPS` and realtime-blocking constraints on every other collector. Tuned `RECON_SPIDERFOOT_WORKERS` 2->1, `MAIGRET_NUM_REQUESTS` 20->10, `GITHUB_MAX_COMMITS_PER_REPO`/`GITHUB_MAX_BRANCHES_PER_REPO` from unlimited to 1000/50, `GITHUB_QUOTA_PUSHER_BATCH_SIZE` 250->100. Added a new `COLLECTOR_STARTUP_DELAY_SECONDS`-driven per-container stagger (`src/worker/__init__.py::_source_startup_delay`, additive to the pre-existing intra-container `index*3.0` spread) to 8 services, spreading first-cycle load across a 5-90s window instead of everything hitting Postgres/network at once on a cold start; realtime-blocking sources (whatsapp/telegram/beeper) deliberately excluded. Verified live: recreated all 8 affected services + spiderfoot (profile-gated, needed an explicit `--profile recon up -d --force-recreate` since a plain `up -d` silently skips profile-scoped services), confirmed 30/30 containers healthy (down from 31 - the merge), spiderfoot command line shows `--workers 1`, spiderfoot/lowrisk/combined exposure/Lemon8 worker memory all reasonable (6.7%/5.7%/22.1% of their caps) post-restart. Honest caveat: host free-memory stayed volatile (3.6%-7.3% fluctuating, non-monotonic) during the ~5 min observation window post-change - consistent with the session-long pattern of a genuinely tight, multi-tenant, multi-agent-active host rather than a regression from these specific changes, but this is NOT a fully-solved/stable state and should keep being watched via the `HostMemoryAlertHandler` added in the prior entry.

- 2026-09-28: Operator explicitly un-held-back the 2 previously-deferred levers and asked for the website collector's timeout to be resolved too. Verified WEBSITE_TARGET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS's own code default is 1500s (src/collectors/website/__init__.py:516) - the compose override had it dialled all the way down to 45s, directly explaining the earlier-found 91.9% deferral rate; raised to 240s (within the recommended 180-300s range, well below the code's own more generous default). Applied the 2 held-back levers: GITHUB_SPIDER_CONCURRENCY 3->1 (combined Strava/search worker) and an explicit MAIGRET_TOP_SITES override 300(implicit default)->100 (spiderfoot/maigret). All 3 verified live via `docker inspect`/container env after a `--profile recon --force-recreate` on collector_website, combined Strava/search worker, and spiderfoot (profile-gated services need the explicit profile flag or a plain `up -d` silently skips them - same gotcha as the last pass). 30/30 containers stayed healthy throughout. Also did the same footprint-reduction scope analysis on the sibling unifiedanalyzer repo (separate git remote, C:\unifiedanalyzer) per operator request - see that repo's own .agents/JOURNAL.md for the full writeup; summary: found zero startup-stagger mechanism there at all (unlike this repo) and added one (scheduler 30s/face_worker 60s/stream_alert_worker 15s), explicitly rejected 2 tempting-looking changes after verifying primary source (a face_worker+scheduler merge blocked by a documented FAISS/OpenMP deadlock history, and a MEDIA_PDF_WRITE_WORKERS reduction that turned out to be a deliberate I/O-latency-hiding pool, not a CPU fan-out risk).

2026-10-11: Began the approved collector overhaul on branch feat/collector-overhaul. Existing main history already had GitHub isolation, five profile services, default-on SpiderFoot, and the idle production collector removal; this pass completes the remaining Compose deduplication, PayPal gate retirement, and browser_ingest naming/documentation work. Hostname placeholder for durable state: dev-host-3.example.
2026-10-11: Overhaul defaults recorded: (1) use native Compose YAML anchors instead of a generator; (2) keep GitHub on the existing collector image/context but isolate it as collector_github; (3) split profile probes as Snapchat, PayPal, Airbnb, Bluesky, and Pinterest; (4) enable recon in both production and standalone dev Compose; (5) remove the PayPal flag entirely and make the public probe unconditional; (6) name the service browser_ingest, retain the Python package src.bridges.ig_ingest for compatibility, and retire only the idle --all service while preserving the CLI option.

## 2026-10-11 - WA Bridge Fix, Telegram Spider, Backup Cleanup (subagent)
- Fixed WA bridges (unifiedcollector_wa_bridge_1/2): root cause was bind mount overlaying /app/build with empty host dir + permissions on /app/auth_info. Fixed by building TS on host and chowning auth dir to UID 100. Both now healthy, QR code available at dashboard :8700.
- TELEGRAM_SPIDER_ACCOUNTS: Removed "placeholder" restriction. Empty value means no restriction (all workers can spider) per _is_spider_allowed logic. Updated docker-compose.yml to allow empty default.
- Deleted migration backups: removed 6.4GB of .dump and .tar.gz files from a local external volume. Live data untouched.
- Log review: Transient "database does not exist" errors during startup (recovered). faiss AVX2 fallback is normal. Minor: onboard_bot Telegram timeout, scheduler notification formatting.

2026-10-13: Resolved YouTube bot-check in UnifiedCollector. Cross-project survey (musicstream, healthkaki-pov, unifiedcollector) revealed the root cause: yt-dlp invokes save_cookies() upon completion, raising PermissionError when accessing the container's read-only mounted cookie file (host uid 1000 vs container collector uid 1001), causing yt-dlp exit code 1 with stderr matching cookie warning regexes, misclassified as a restricted/bot failure. Furthermore, _usable_cookie_file() had permanently memoized an empty string at boot time when the cookie file was initially absent. Fixed by invalidating empty cookie cache on file appearance and adding _create_writable_cookie_snapshot() to copy cookies into ephemeral operation tempdirs across video downloads, thumbnail collection, transcript extraction, comment dumping, and playlist scraping. Verified with live metadata probe on public video jNQXAC9IVRw in unifiedcollector_collector_youtube (RC 0, formats extracted, zero bot challenge). Bulk downloads avoided.

2026-10-14: Rate-limit audit uncovered a TikTok profile challenge wall recurring at 02:05:46 and 02:35:52 UTC, exactly at the fixed 1,800-second cooldown expiry (the latter was status 200, not an HTTP 429). Root cause was a fixed persisted event cooldown and a direct profile-metadata path that did not guard active cooldown state. A bounded pre-fix mock reproduced a request despite an active cooldown. Added persistent exponential profile cooldowns (30-minute base, 24-hour cap, 7-day streak memory), Retry-After delta/date parsing, restore from service cursors, and a pre-request cooldown guard. Added regression tests for challenge backoff, cooldown suppression/restoration, and Retry-After. Python compilation, Compose validation, and bounded mocked probes pass; pytest is unavailable in host/image. The prior 15-minute check had no actual HTTP-429/FloodWait logs or status-429 rows, but its one status-200 challenge row means the table-growth criterion needs a fresh soak after deployment.

2026-10-14: Item 3 Airbnb investigation found the feeder had created 6,311 cross-platform rows already marked 404, 881 errors, and 810 pending, with zero direct Airbnb user-profile links in `discovered_links`. A pre-fix mock confirmed the collector called `/users/profile/{id}`; one bounded public numeric-ID request to `/users/show/{id}` returned 200 but no profile payload markers. Airbnb Community documents numeric user IDs in `/users/show/{id}` (https://community.withairbnb.com/t5/Help-with-your-business/Can-t-find-my-User-ID/td-p/2138665). Changed feeder to numeric direct Airbnb links only, removed cross-platform Airbnb fan-out, corrected the URL, and changed unparsed 200 app shells from false 404s to errors. Added regression tests. 2026-10-14: Confirmed Bluesky profile root cause before fixing: live `bluesky_profiles` has 0 rows; the feeder has no resolver call, filters bare names, and the prior filter change removed Bluesky cross-platform candidates. The live queue holds 8,102 historical rows (6,769 bare; all 8,102 are already 404/error), `social_users` has 114,976 distinct eligible handles, and there are currently no direct Bluesky profile links. Bluesky collection therefore has no valid incoming DID targets. The feed will resolve cross-platform bare usernames as `<username>.bsky.social` through `com.atproto.identity.resolveHandle`, cache definitive misses outside the probe queue, and enqueue only resolved DIDs; direct qualified handles will also be resolved first.

2026-10-16 00:21 UTC Postgres performance research: A fresh `docker stats` point sample showed unifiedcollector Postgres at 105.73% CPU and 2.3/4 GiB memory; `musicstream-daemon` was at 84.99%, analyzer Postgres at 36.19%, and the face worker at 44.33% CPU/94.96% memory cap. Together with earlier 123–373% Postgres CPU samples, slow statements, parallel data-file reads, 85GB cumulative temp writes, and frequent timed checkpoints, this indicates real DB pressure but not that Postgres is the sole host bottleneck. PG16 docs reviewed: `pg_stat_statements` identifies expensive normalized SQL but needs `shared_preload_libraries` and restart; `track_io_timing`/`pg_stat_io` help distinguish I/O from CPU (measure timing overhead); `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` and sampled `auto_explain` can reveal plans/spills, with overhead and parameter-log privacy controls. The large temp-byte count is cumulative, so compare fixed-window deltas. Candidate tune-ups after attribution: optimize the top SQL/index/plans first; consider session-scoped `work_mem` only for confirmed spill-heavy statements; test a longer checkpoint interval only if logs/metrics confirm the 5-minute timed checkpoints cause write pressure; consider pooler only if connection churn/active backends become a measured issue (57 of 200 were present in the earlier sample). `shared_buffers=1GB` is already 25% of the 4GiB cap and global `work_mem=16MB` can multiply by concurrent operations, so neither should be raised blindly. No DB settings or services changed.
